Tea Before the Revolution","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0078","paragraphs":["Cassian had been offered tea, legal advice from the company, and the opportunity to improve his own recollection. He had accepted the tea. The man who delivered the other offers seemed to regard this selective appreciation as an offense. \"You are accustomed to cooperation on your own terms,\" he said. Cassian looked at the locked door. \"I have lately been introduced to yours.\"","The interviewer was not a neuroscientist. A qualified technician would arrive to prepare the apparatus, and no one had yet fitted Cassian with a paired contact cuff. That delay was useful. The company wanted to recover the location of documents he had handled recently; it could not open his childhood like a credit-card account. He knew the limits because he had helped establish them in testimony. Knowing them did not make the prospect of another interference bearable.","He kept his attention on concrete things: the table's edge, the clock, the door to the next room. His wrists hurt where the guard had restrained him during the transfer. His mind was conscious and his thoughts remained his. The claim that he could protect Mara by surrendering their work was indefensible. There were copies beyond his reach now. He had been very careful to arrange that, even when he still possessed an office and a title on the door.","A trolley rattled outside. The interviewer turned, irritated at the interruption. A woman in a cap entered carrying a tray. She placed two cups on the table and kept her face lowered. Cassian recognized the small scar by her thumb before he saw her eyes. He had kissed that hand in a kitchen where the worst foreseeable disaster had been Rowan stealing their breakfast. The memory was so bright that he had to lower his gaze to keep it from appearing on his face.","\"I asked for water,\" he said.","\"There is water,\" she replied, setting the jug beside his cup. \"Somebody wrote the order badly.\"","He almost smiled. The interviewer asked her to leave. Mara pointed at a spill beneath his chair and said the cleaning supervisor had already complained about dirty floors. The man looked down. It was a small mark, but the possibility of being blamed for it was enough. He stood, gathered his papers, and went to the adjacent room to make a call. The guard remained at the door.","Fen appeared behind the trolley with an expression of continual disappointment. She told the guard that a delivery had blocked the corridor. It would only take a moment to move. He left the room with her, keeping the door open. Mara stepped around the table as though she intended to clean its edge. For the first time since the depot, nothing stood between her and Cassian except the work of keeping her voice steady.","\"You look terrible,\" she whispered.","\"You have dressed to match.\"","She touched his cheek. He caught her wrist lightly, turned his face into her palm, and let himself breathe. \"I thought you might bring a lawyer.\" \"I brought an unreasonable woman. The lawyer is drafting an appreciation of our methods.\" His smile was thin with fatigue and entirely his. She wanted to stay there until the city turned kind. Instead she looked at the cuffs on the chair and the door beyond him.","\"The crew entrance opens to the east gallery,\" he murmured. \"The rear hall is closed from the local control room. Three guards on this floor. The deputy is downstairs.\"","\"The window?\"","\"New safety glass. Old frame.\"","She looked at it. \"I like the old frame.\"","\"I thought you might.\"","The guard returned before she could answer. Mara dropped the cloth to the floor and bent after it. Cassian asked the man whether an independent adviser had been contacted. His tone was precise, tiresome, and apparently incapable of running out of questions. The guard settled into the familiar effort of refusing them. Mara left with the tray, carrying more information than the interviewer had obtained in two hours.","In the supply room, she spoke briefly into the small radio. The old observation link was carrying their voices with a faint hiss. Ivo could hear her from the service yard; Anika was checking the local supply arrangement. Their intended route through the gallery still existed, but the locked rear hall had added a complication. They would need more than a clever pair of hands inside. They would need a way to keep the guards from sealing every exit at once.","Leah had located the cleaning crew's supervisor, who was willing to explain the shift pattern. The establishment had split ordinary staff from the security workforce; neither knew the other's decisions in advance. That bias toward separate control could be a weakness. A properly timed delivery would add to the crowd in the service yard. Rook could provide it. A postponed laundry pickup gave the van a reason to wait rather than disappear.","Mara checked Fen's expression. \"You can leave now.\" Fen shook her head. \"My niece told you where I work. She did not tell you I am tame.\" She picked up the folded cloths and went out to finish the floor. Her composure was more persuasive than a declaration of bravery. She had done this job through bad management, bad weather, and one memorable dispute about the size of a cleaning tub. She intended to outlast this employer too.","The supervisor called them back to the corridor. A technician had arrived early. Behind him, an attendant pushed a case bearing the company's flagship mark. Mara's belly tightened when she recognized the model of cuff inside. Its contact surfaces would sit directly against the subject's skin. Communication from the central station could activate a paired node, but could not turn every object in the building into one. The simple physical requirement gave their rescue a time limit: the technician had to be kept from finishing his work.","Mara stepped in front of the trolley. \"The room has not been cleared.\" The technician stopped. The interviewer, returning from his call, demanded to know why cleaning staff were suddenly regulating the schedule. Fen looked at the floor. \"Because a man who asked for a spotless room has spilled something in it.\" \"That is tea.\" \"Then you may walk through it without my help.\" The technician, more concerned with keeping his equipment dry than winning the argument, waited.","The delay lasted half a minute. A guard escorted Fen toward the stair to fetch the supervisor. Mara was left beside the equipment case with the technician and the interviewer. She could not carry it away without being seen. She could, however, point to the seal on its side. The printed number did not correspond to the one on the accompanying form. It was a real discrepancy. She had noticed it because her whole world had narrowed to things that might give Cassian another minute.","The technician opened the paperwork. \"Where did you see that?\"","\"On the label.\"","He checked it twice, then swore. The interviewer told him to proceed. The technician refused. Pairing a contact node under the wrong recorded identity would spoil the very material he had been sent to collect. He was not objecting to the detention; he was objecting to an error that would be attached to his own name. For the moment, Mara did not require a saint. She required him to stop.","At the far end of the corridor, the deputy appeared. He had come back from an inspection with another guard behind him. His eyes passed over the equipment, the open form, and the woman who had drawn everyone's attention to it. He looked at Mara's face. Something in his expression changed. The depot had contained a courier in a cap. The archive had contained a cleaner. He might not yet have put the same woman into both places, but he was beginning to enjoy the attempt.","\"Your name?\" he asked.","Mara lifted the tray. \"I am expected downstairs.\"","\"Your name.\"","She dropped the tray into the sink beside her. The clang was magnificent. The deputy flinched toward the noise; Mara shoved the empty equipment trolley across his route and ran. A hand caught her sleeve at the doorway. She slipped out of the loose coat, leaving him with a beautifully tailored piece of laundry, and took the service stair two steps at a time. From inside the interview room, Cassian heard the crash and stood so sharply that his chair struck the table.","Mara reached the lower hall and nearly collided with Fen. Two guards were questioning the older woman. Mara changed direction, snatched a wheeled laundry basket, and sent it rolling between them. Sheets spilled across the floor. Fen stepped neatly out of reach, caught Mara's hand, and ran with her toward the rear passage. It was an efficient partnership built in less than an hour and conducted without a word of committee business.","The rear door opened onto the service yard. Rook's van was there, its back doors wide, its floor covered with laundry that had not belonged to it that morning. Mara pushed Fen inside. The guard behind them reached the vehicle just as Rook released the brake. He struck the door with his palm and stumbled back. Nobody fell beneath the wheels. Mara slammed it shut, and the van swung into the street with all the decorum of a furious housewife taking clothes to wash.","For two blocks, nobody spoke. Then Fen began to laugh. \"I shall have to apologise for the coat.\" Mara stared at her before a breathless, unwilling laugh escaped as well. The laughter did not reduce the failure. Cassian was still inside. The device was closer to him. But Fen was out, and they had the room, the frame, the guards, and the technician's delay. Those things were not of small account.","At the safe room, Mara spread the sketch in front of Ivo. Her voice shook until she began pointing. The window's frame was old. The gallery ran along the exterior wall. The apparatus had not been paired because of the mismatched record. Anika could explain the local panel; Leah could get the remaining staff clear if a repair team needed access. They would not repeat the same approach and hope the guards had forgotten her face. They would build the next attempt around what they now knew.","June returned with a copy of the deputy's public transfer notice. It had been issued to clear the wing before morning. Cassian's interview would not last indefinitely; whatever Selene wanted next was already moving through the system. Mara took the notice and marked the time beside the room. \"We go before that,\" she said. Nobody told her it was impossible. Ivo reached for the building plan, and the whole table moved closer."],"questions":[{"type":"Cause / motive","stem":"Why does the technician initially refuse to begin the contact procedure?","options":["He believes that cleaning staff have permanent authority over the interview wing.","He has decided to release every detainee on ethical grounds.","The equipment label does not match the accompanying record, creating a data-assignment and accountability problem.","He has concluded that no such equipment can work."],"answer":2,"explanation":"技术员发现设备标签与随附记录不符，担心采集数据归档与个人责任，不是突然成为反抗者。错误不会让机器生物学上失效，更不是女主获得永久指挥权。","evidence":[26],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1801,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0078\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>6. Cause / motive</p><p><strong>Why does the technician initially refuse to begin the contact procedure?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. He believes that cleaning staff have permanent authority over the interview wing.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. The Woman Who Came Through the Window","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0079","paragraphs":["The rescue team arrived at the north annex in a dairy van. Rook objected to the vehicle on professional grounds. Its horn played a tune, its side showed a smiling cow, and its proprietor had asked her not to damage the ice-cream compartment. \"I spent twenty years avoiding humiliating uniforms,\" she said. \"Now I am commanding the milk sector.\" Leah put the route sheet on the seat. \"An advancement. The crew is much better behaved.\"","There were three tasks. Anika and Ivo would enter the service enclosure as the repair team already requested by Fen's supervisor. Leah would get the ordinary staff clear of the rear wing. Mara would reach the interview room from the outside gallery. Rook would keep transport ready. June would watch the public exits and report changes. Their capability lay in the fit between these pieces. Nobody, including Mara, could substitute for the whole arrangement.","The service gallery ran behind an old architectural panel that the national trust had preserved during a previous renovation. It was not a secret passage built for burglars. It was a maintenance route badly matched to the additions around it. The archaeologist's photographs had revealed what the current administration had forgotten to label. Mara checked the final drawing, tightened the line around her waist, and climbed onto the platform behind the repair van.","\"There is a rule about falling,\" Ivo told her.","\"Does it involve not doing it?\"","\"You are astonishingly qualified.\"","She grinned at him. It was the first expression since the depot that looked entirely like her. He helped her over the rail, then turned back to his own work. The system did not require a lone hero. It required a woman who could climb, engineers who knew what they were touching, and a driver who was already threatening the cow on her van with a funeral.","Inside, Cassian watched the technician examine the corrected identity form. The man had returned with an appropriate unit, a fresh contact cuff, and an air of injured professional importance. An automated console displayed the beginning of a pairing procedure. Cassian had avoided looking at the cuff for as long as he could. Now he looked directly at it. Fear was becoming more manageable when it had a shape, less so when he allowed it to populate every corner of his imagination.","The interviewer told him that cooperation would shorten the process. \"It might also improve our understanding of your account,\" he added. Cassian rested both hands on the table. \"My account was recorded at the hearing.\" \"You have been a difficult respondent.\" \"I did not realize agreement was the criterion.\" The man dismissed the objection with a gesture. The guard moved behind Cassian's chair.","On the gallery, Mara crawled past the blocked air-conditioning vent and found the window. Its new pane sat inside the old frame, just as Cassian had said. The recent work was finely finished on the visible side. The exterior had been left with a temporary support awaiting replacement. Anika inspected it from below, then gave a brief signal. A section could be lifted safely if the frame was held. It was not a request to smash glass over the people inside.","Mara worked the outer fastening while Ivo took the load on the support. The frame moved by a fraction, then stopped. Old paint clung along its edge. Her fingers were cold and the surface gave her almost nothing to grip. She changed her position, set both feet firmly, and tried again with patient pressure. The gap widened. Through it, she could see Cassian's shoulder and the guard reaching for his wrist.","A radio in her ear hissed. \"Rear staff clear,\" Leah said. \"The deputy has gone to inspect the lift.\" That departure had been deliberate. Fen's supervisor had refused to sign another report about its failure unless someone with authority watched it fail. It was not the most glamorous part of the mission. It had moved the right man away from the right door. Mara felt the world narrow to a pane of glass and a pair of hands she loved.","The technician fitted the first band. Before its second contact could close, the console went dark. Anika had isolated the supply to the interview equipment, leaving the public exit and the ordinary lights in operation. There was no mysterious assault on cyberspace, no universal cancel button. There was one local apparatus that could not begin its work without power. The technician swore and turned toward the wall panel.","Mara lifted the window clear. Ivo held its frame while she swung inside, landing behind the curtain with a soft thud. Cassian turned. His expression changed from alarm to a joy so fierce that she nearly forgot the guard. The man saw her next and reached for his radio. She caught his arm with the hanging curtain, pulled it across his hands, and drove her shoulder into him. He staggered against the desk. Cassian rose with the chair still fastened to one wrist and knocked the radio out of reach.","The fight lasted seconds and felt untidy. Mara took a blow against her upper arm. The guard caught her sleeve and tore it. Cassian used his free hand to turn the chair away from her; its weight caught the guard's shin. Mara twisted clear, shoved the empty trolley between them, and sent the man backward into the curtain. The technician backed toward the door with both palms open. He had come to supervise an apparatus, not participate in combat.","\"Open the restraint,\" Mara told him.","He looked at Cassian, then at the dark console. He opened it. Cassian tore the unpaired cuff from his wrist and put it on the table as though touching it any longer might become unbearable. Mara caught his face between her hands. He was here. Awake, angry, tired, and here. \"I disliked the room,\" she said. He kissed her with no resemblance to a measured response. \"I had begun to share your opinion.\"","A shout in the corridor broke the moment. The guard had recovered enough to call for help. Mara pushed the door closed and set the trolley against it. The window was one route out, but Cassian had seen two other detained people in the next room. \"They are still here,\" he said. She followed his glance to the adjoining door. The company had not acquired ownership of them by putting one more locked panel in their way.","The technician opened the side room. A municipal clerk and a Mexican visiting engineer stared at Mara as though a woman arriving through a window was the final collapse of normal administration. \"We are leaving,\" she told them. \"Can you climb?\" The clerk said no. Her ankle was injured. Mara did not tell her to discover hidden athletic gifts under pressure. She called Ivo and changed the route.","The local power isolation had brought two maintenance staff to the corridor. Fen's supervisor was with them, demanding that the deputy stop shouting at people who were trying to work. Leah moved the laundry trolley into the hall. Its folded blankets made a seat for the clerk. The engineer supported her while Cassian took the other side. Mara opened their door into a noisy argument about safety, an argument the supervisor had spent years preparing to win.","The deputy spotted Cassian immediately. He pushed past the maintenance workers. Mara took the folded screen beside the doorway and opened it directly across his path. He hit its fabric, swore, and shoved it down. By then Cassian and the others had reached the service stair. Mara followed, caught the rail, and slid along the short landing faster than the man behind her could turn. The door closed between them with a sound she would remember with great affection.","At the lower turn, the injured clerk cried out as the trolley bumped. Cassian stopped to steady her. Mara looked up at the approaching footsteps and then down toward the rear exit. \"Now,\" she called into the radio. Outside, the dairy van's horn began its cheerful tune. Three delivery workers entered through the rear passage carrying an unwieldy freezer they had been asked to retrieve. It occupied the full width of the corridor, and they were in no haste to discover it was wanted elsewhere.","The rescuers came through beside it one at a time. Rook had the van doors open. The engineer climbed in first; Cassian lifted the clerk onto the seat without waiting to have his bruised wrist admired. Mara leaped after them. Anika and Ivo were already in the second vehicle. Leah shut the rear gate just as the deputy reached it. \"You cannot monopolize the loading area,\" she told him. \"We have a schedule.\"","Rook drove. In the mirror, the deputy dwindled beside the smiling cow painted on the gate sign. Mara began to laugh and discovered that she was crying at the same time. Cassian caught her against him. \"I am here,\" he said. The words did not claim that everything was all right. They named the one fact she had needed so badly that the rest of the world had become its frame.","They stopped at an old workshop rather than Mercy House, giving the hospital no additional reason to face a search. Noor came with a small medical bag and examined the freed people. Cassian had bruising, exhaustion, and a headache. The contact procedure had not begun; he remained clear and able to speak for himself. He accepted the examination with less grace than he had accepted the dairy van. Mara sat where he could reach her hand and pretended not to enjoy the reversal.","When Noor finished, she gave them five minutes before the others came back. Mara leaned beside Cassian on the narrow couch. His arm went around her. \"You came through a window,\" he said. \"You had expressed a preference for the frame.\" He laughed, then pulled her closer. She could feel his heart under her cheek, steady enough to make her close her eyes. \"Next time,\" he murmured, \"we shall choose a room with better exits.\" \"Next time, we are going on a quiet date.\" \"An ambitious benchmark.\"","Outside, Ivo and Anika unfolded the maintenance plan. The first mission had brought Cassian back. The next would have to make sure Selene could not keep choosing who vanished behind a controlled door. Mara heard them arguing over the upper tier of the tower and kissed Cassian once more before she stood. When he rose beside her, his hand remained at the small of her back. The city had not been won. It had recovered two people who intended to fight for it together."],"questions":[{"type":"Cause / humane choice","stem":"Why does Mara change the escape route after reaching Cassian?","options":["Another detainee cannot climb because of an injured ankle, so the group needs an alternative route.","The team has lost every vehicle waiting outside.","The technician has proved that no corridor is being watched.","Cassian refuses to leave unless the deputy apologizes."],"answer":0,"explanation":"相邻房间还有两名被留置者，其中一人脚踝受伤不能爬窗，女主据此改变方案，没有要求对方临时表现出不现实的体能。其他选项均无文本依据。","evidence":[19],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1784,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0079\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>7.
